# United Arab Emirates: migration guide

How to go to the United Arab Emirates (UAE) on a Bangladeshi passport, visa by visa, step by step. \[3\] is an official source; \[N3\] is a news, NGO, research or guide source, listed under "Needs an official source"; a year, like \[N3 (2022)\], marks a source older than 2025. "Need to know" marks a point no source has yet confirmed. "You'll need:" lists what to have before starting a procedure.

Tk is the Bangladeshi taka (1 lakh is Tk 100,000); AED is the UAE dirham, converted throughout at AED1 = about Tk 34, the rate on 8 November 2026 \[[1](#o1), [2](#o2)\]. BMET (Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) is the Bangladesh government agency that registers people who want to work abroad and gives them emigration clearance \[[3](#o3), [4](#o4)\]. In the UAE, MOHRE (Ministry of Human Resources and Emiratisation) issues work permits, and two authorities issue visas and residence: GDRFA (General Directorate of Identity and Foreigners Affairs) for Dubai, and ICP (Federal Authority for Identity, Citizenship, Customs and Port Security) for the other six emirates (Abu Dhabi, Sharjah, Ajman, Umm Al Quwain, Ras Al Khaimah and Fujairah) \[[5](#o5), [6](#o6)\]. Bangladeshis need a visa arranged in advance to enter the UAE for any purpose; there is no visa on arrival for them \[[7](#o7), [8](#o8)\].

## Background

**In one minute.** A UAE job starts with a MOHRE offer letter and work permit issued to a UAE company for a named worker, and the law makes the employer pay every recruitment cost \[[9](#o9), [6](#o6), [10 (2021)](#o10)\]. In practice most visas come through a relative already in the UAE or are bought and resold through brokers and agencies, and "free visas" and partner visas sell the right to live in the UAE with no real job behind them \[[N1](#n1), [N2](#n2), [N3 (2019)](#n3)\]. The government cap is Tk 1,07,780, but Dubai Taxi drivers pay at least Tk 3.5 lakh, and Middle East jobs in general cost Tk 5 to 7 lakh \[[11 (2019)](#o11), [N4](#n4), [N1](#n1)\]. Since July 2024, when Bangladeshis were jailed for protesting in UAE streets, visit visas and most work visas for Bangladeshis have been restricted, with no published ban \[[12 (2024)](#o12), [N5](#n5), [N6](#n6)\]. Some work visas still go through: BMET cleared 23,044 workers for the UAE from 1 January to 7 November 2026, but only 63 in the first week of November \[[4](#o4), [13](#o13)\]. Since July 2026 the UAE has been cancelling the residence visas of Bangladeshis who went home on leave, about 5,000 by mid-October, with no formal reason given \[[N7](#n7), [N8](#n8), [N9](#n9)\]. The visa ties you to one employer, who can cancel it online without telling you \[[14](#o14), [15](#o15), [N10](#n10)\].

**Is it open now, and who can go?**

Partly open: new work visas for Bangladeshis have been restricted since July 2024, and since July 2026 the UAE has been cancelling the residence visas of Bangladeshis who went home on leave \[[N5](#n5), [N7](#n7)\].

- **Status on 8 November 2026:** no published UAE ban exists, but the Expatriates' Welfare Minister says family, student, tourist and employment visas, and transfers of a visa from one company to another, have all been restricted or stalled since July 2024 \[[16](#o16), [N11](#n11)\]. Some work visas are issued: BMET cleared 23,044 workers for the UAE from 1 January to 7 November 2026, over 4,000 a month in July to September, then 951 in October and 63 in the first week of November \[[4](#o4), [13](#o13)\].
- **Visas cancelled while home on leave (July 2026 onward):** notices arrive by email, text message or through the kafil (sponsor), often with no reason; some people learned only at check-in, when the airline system refused them a boarding pass \[[N12](#n12), [N13](#n13), [N10](#n10), [N14](#n14)\]. People whose visas still had more than a year to run were hit \[[N15](#n15)\].

  The count kept rising:
  - 626 on 30 July 2026 (Foreign Affairs State Minister Shama Obaed, who said Pakistanis, Egyptians, Filipinos and Nepalis were also affected) \[[N16](#n16)\];
  - 985 sent to the UAE by diplomatic note by 3 August, out of about 2,500 emails the embassy received \[[N9](#n9), [N7](#n7)\];
  - 4,000 on 6 September (State Minister Nurul Haque Nur) \[[N7](#n7)\];
  - 4,800 by mid-September \[[N12](#n12), [N13](#n13)\];
  - 5,000 on 13 October (Law Minister Md Asaduzzaman, who added that many renewals were pending) \[[N8](#n8)\];
  - about 6,600, the figure protesters gave outside Probashi Kallyan Bhaban on 7 October \[[N17](#n17)\].
- **The reasons given:** the UAE has given no formal explanation \[[N9](#n9)\]. The state minister says the UAE cites internal security \[[N13](#n13)\]. A senior embassy official listed possible causes: the company hired over its quota, the job on the visa does not match the real work, Wage Protection System (WPS, the UAE's electronic salary-payment system) violations, company licence problems, a labourer working on an investor visa, social media posts against UAE law, and criminal or disciplinary cases \[[N9](#n9)\]. Bangladesh's ministers warn workers not to film sensitive events or post them online \[[N16](#n16), [N11](#n11)\].
- **What Bangladesh has done:** the embassy collects cases by email and forwards them to the UAE; being on its list does not guarantee reinstatement, because only the UAE government can reactivate a visa \[[N13](#n13), [N9](#n9)\]. Bangladesh asked the UAE to reconsider the decision and re-examine the cancelled visas \[[N13](#n13)\]. On 7 September 2026, affected returnees organised as "Amra Remittance Joddha" (We Are Remittance Fighters) demanded monthly allowances of Tk 15,000-20,000, jobs in other countries and high-level talks with the UAE \[[N7](#n7)\].

> **Need to know**
> - Why did BMET clearances for the UAE fall from 4,442 in September 2026 to 951 in October: a new UAE restriction, flight disruption, or a pause in BMET processing?
> - Has the UAE reinstated any of the cancelled residence visas, and through which office (ICP, GDRFA or MOHRE)?

**Visa by visa, November 2026** (details in each visa section):
- **New work visa, low-skilled:** restricted since July 2024; a ministry official said none were issued in 2025, though BMET cleared 13,750 workers that year; the largest titles were cleaner (1,691), driver (1,252), accountant (1,157), helper (1,051) and manager (986) \[[N5](#n5), [17](#o17)\].
- **New work visa, named employer recruiting in bulk:** moving in 2026. Dubai Taxi Company plans to hire 6,000 Bangladeshi drivers this year, and the largest 2026 clearance categories are construction helper (5,935), driver (2,591), cleaner (1,960), mason (1,756) and steel fixer (1,503) \[[N4](#n4), [4](#o4)\]. Bangladeshis are already the largest group among Dubai Taxi's 15,000 drivers, about 8,000, and the company has taken about 3,000 from Bangladesh since 2017 \[[N4](#n4)\]. 788 workers were cleared as delivery bike riders in 2026 \[[4](#o4)\]. The government says it has begun the process of reopening the UAE labour market \[[18](#o18)\].
- **Skilled work visa:** MOHRE reactivated its online system for skilled Bangladeshi workers in May 2025 \[[N18](#n18)\].
- **Visit visa:** the UAE Embassy in Dhaka issued 30 to 50 a day in May 2025; by October 2025 travel agents called tourist and transit visas "almost zero" \[[N18](#n18), [N19](#n19)\].
- **Residence visa of people now in Bangladesh on leave:** at risk of automatic cancellation \[[N7](#n7), [N10](#n10)\].

**The channels.**
- **A named employer recruiting through a licensed agency:** Dubai Taxi's 2026 drive runs through three agencies, Al Anas Overseas, VIP Global and M Bahar Overseas, with screening at the Bangladesh-Korea Technical Training Centre from 18 May 2026 \[[N4](#n4)\].
- **A visa found by a relative in the UAE:** the most common route; workers say it is safer than agents \[[N1](#n1), [N20 (2019)](#n20)\].
- **Partner or investor visa:** a residence visa tied to a trade licence, sold as a way in when labour visas closed; working for anyone else on it is illegal ("How a job reaches you") \[[N3 (2019)](#n3)\].

**Who qualifies.** Whoever the employer's MOHRE work permit names: the UAE portal says a genuine job comes with a MOHRE offer letter signed by the employer's authorised manager \[[6](#o6)\]. Dubai Taxi drivers must communicate in English \[[N4](#n4)\].

**Size.**
- More than 12 lakh Bangladeshis live in the UAE by unofficial estimates, the third-largest foreign community after Indians and Pakistanis \[[N12](#n12), [N19](#n19)\]. A private estimate puts them at 0.84 million, against 4.36 million Indians, 1.9 million Pakistanis, 0.78 million Filipinos and 0.36 million Nepalis \[[N21](#n21)\].
- BMET clearances for the UAE: 98,201 in 2023 (4th destination), 47,158 in 2024 (5th), 13,750 in 2025 (6th) and 23,044 in 2026 up to 7 November (6th, behind Saudi Arabia, Singapore, Qatar, the Maldives and Kuwait) \[[19](#o19), [20](#o20), [21](#o21), [22](#o22), [17](#o17), [23](#o23), [4](#o4), [24](#o24)\]. Before the curbs about 5,000 to 6,000 a month went \[[N19](#n19)\]. From 2004 to May 2025 BMET counts 2,207,991 Bangladeshis sent to the UAE, with a peak of 419,355 in 2008 \[[25](#o25)\]; Saudi Arabia took 38.85 lakh from 2004 to April 2024 \[[N22 (2024)](#n22)\]. In January to September 2026 UAE clearances more than doubled while Saudi Arabia's fell 45.5%, Qatar's 37.4% and Kuwait's 33.4% \[[N23](#n23)\].
- Remittances from the UAE: USD 4,583.9 million in FY2025-26, of USD 35,589.39 million in total; in July to September 2026 the UAE was the third source after Saudi Arabia and the UK \[[26](#o26), [27](#o27)\].

**What closed it before.**
- **2012 to 2020:** the UAE largely stopped labour visas for Bangladeshis from 2012; the Ministry links this possibly to concerns about recruitment practices and the UAE's policy of giving jobs to its own nationals; migration fell until 2021 \[[11 (2019)](#o11), [N5](#n5), [N6](#n6)\].
- **April 2018:** Bangladesh and the UAE signed an MoU (memorandum of understanding, a government-to-government agreement) on recruiting domestic workers, under which the UAE agreed to recruit Bangladeshi women and men in 19 job categories; flows still fell until 2021 \[[28 (2018)](#o28), [11 (2019)](#o11), [N6](#n6)\].
- **2021 to mid-2024:** flows rose again on various visas, to 101,000 in 2022 and 98,000 in 2023 \[[N6](#n6), [N24](#n24)\].
- **19 July 2024:** Bangladeshis marched in UAE streets in support of the quota reform movement. Protests without permission are banned. On 21 July a federal court sentenced 57: three to life, 53 to ten years, one (who had entered illegally) to eleven years, all to be deported afterwards \[[N25 (2024)](#n25), [12 (2024)](#o12)\]. Recording and sharing videos of the gathering counted against them \[[12 (2024)](#o12)\]. Visa applications for Bangladeshis were blocked from 22 July 2024 \[[N24](#n24), [N6](#n6)\].
- **3 September 2024:** the UAE President pardoned them and ordered deportation; 131 more were arrested and 188 freed in all, most flown home in November and December 2024, jobless \[[29 (2024)](#o29), [N26 (2024)](#n26), [N27 (2024)](#n27)\]. Their fingerprints were kept, and a Foreign Ministry director said they may not get into the UAE or other Gulf states again \[[N26 (2024)](#n26)\].
- **2025:** limited reopening (30 to 50 visit visas a day, skilled visas, 500 security-guard visas granted and 1,000 more approved) after the chief adviser's special envoy lobbied; the envoy was told over 25% of the UAE's visa and residency violators are Bangladeshis \[[N18](#n18), [N19](#n19)\]. Government insiders say recruitment fraud, fake documents and visa violations also led the UAE to tighten screening, and visit visas were curbed because Bangladeshis used them to find work \[[N19](#n19), [N28](#n28)\]; Bangladesh's Foreign Ministry calls the restrictions a UAE policy decision \[[N19](#n19)\]. In January 2026 industry insiders listed the UAE among countries that often reject Bangladeshi visa applications \[[N28](#n28)\].
- **July 2026 onward:** residence visas of people on leave cancelled (above).

**How a job reaches you, and who takes a cut**

A UAE work visa starts with a UAE company that has a MOHRE work permit for a named person; the law makes that employer pay recruitment costs, so the ambassador says a worker should be able to come free \[[6](#o6), [N1](#n1)\]. In practice the visa is bought and sold through layers of middlemen, and the price rises at each hand \[[N1](#n1), [N29 (2022)](#n29)\].

**Who carries the visa.**
- **Relatives:** most workers come through a relative already in the UAE \[[N1](#n1), [N20 (2019)](#n20)\].
- **Agencies and their brokers:** a 2019 study found agents and relatives alike paint the job rosier than it is, and the contract signed in Bangladesh often differs from the one the company holds \[[N20 (2019)](#n20)\].

**Free visa, partner visa, company visa.** Workers sort UAE visas this way:
- **কোম্পানি ভিসা (company visa):** a work permit for a real job with the company that sponsors you; the Dubai Taxi and construction visas of 2026 are this kind \[[N4](#n4), [4](#o4)\].
- **ফ্রি ভিসা (free visa):** no such visa exists in UAE law. Brokers sell a work permit in a company's name with no job behind it; the worker must find work himself, working for anyone else is illegal, the company can cancel the visa at any time, and renewal means paying the broker again \[[N2](#n2)\].
- **পার্টনার/ইনভেস্টর ভিসা (partner or investor visa):** residence as a part-owner of a trade licence, usually one licence for two people plus an Arab partner paid over a lakh taka and a shop or office lease \[[N3 (2019)](#n3)\]. In 2019 brokers charged Tk 6 to 7 lakh, bringing people in on visit visas, though the visa cost less than half that \[[N3 (2019)](#n3)\]. The holder may work only for his own licence: one caught working for an Abu Dhabi company got two months' jail and deportation, and the company was fined AED 50,000 \[[N3 (2019)](#n3)\]. Raids made employers stop hiring partner-visa holders \[[N3 (2019)](#n3)\]. In 2026 the embassy listed "labourer working on an investor visa" among possible reasons for cancellations \[[N9](#n9)\].

**What the clearance data shows.** BMET's own clearance titles for the UAE in 2023 were led by "Manager" (23,300), then "Worker" (20,050), "Accountant" (8,597) and "Labour" (6,341); 2024 had 1,006 cleared as "Partner" \[[19](#o19), [21](#o21)\]. In 2026 construction helpers, drivers, cleaners and masons lead, though "Marketing Manager", "Accountant" and "Sales Manager" still number in the hundreds \[[4](#o4)\]. A visa whose job title does not match the real work is one of the possible reasons the embassy gave for the 2026 cancellations \[[N9](#n9)\].

> **Need to know**
> - Who were the 23,300 Bangladeshis cleared as "Manager" for the UAE in 2023: real managers, partner-visa holders, or labourers on visas with skilled titles bought to pass the restriction?
> - What does a UAE company work visa (construction helper, cleaner, driver) sell for in Dhaka in 2026, and what does a "free visa" or partner visa sell for?
> - Who sells UAE visas to Bangladeshi brokers: the UAE sponsor (kafil) himself, Bangladeshi-owned companies in the UAE, or typing-centre agents?

**The choke point.** Two gates: the UAE deciding whether to issue a visa to a Bangladeshi passport at all, and BMET's emigration clearance (the smart card) \[[N6](#n6), [N29 (2022)](#n29)\]. The second has been sold: a 2022 Ministry inquiry found BMET had issued 6,938 smart cards against UAE permissions for 2,960 workers, sold to manpower traders and brokers for Tk 3 to 4.5 lakh each, and named BMET officials in the ring \[[N29 (2022)](#n29)\]. Workers sent on those cards got neither legal status nor jobs, and many slept in parks \[[N29 (2022)](#n29)\]. In September 2026 a politician alleged that BMET had suspended clearance cards for a week while cards were being issued through back channels for more money \[[N30](#n30)\]. Each agency's record is in the `agencies` folder.

**What you pay, and what you earn and keep**

**The cap.** The government's maximum for the UAE is Tk 1,07,780, set by the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and Overseas Employment in 2017 \[[11 (2019)](#o11)\]. Under UAE law the employer pays recruitment costs (Step 1) \[[6](#o6)\].

**What people really pay.** For Dubai Taxi driver jobs, the state minister said in May 2026 that workers pay at least Tk 3.5 lakh; he asked for Tk 1 lakh \[[N4](#n4)\]. Middle East jobs in general cost Tk 5 to 7 lakh in 2025 \[[N1](#n1)\]. The government survey of 2015-18 migrants (Bangladesh Bureau of Statistics, BBS, with the ILO) found men paid Tk 4.71 lakh on average, unskilled workers Tk 4.78 lakh and skilled Tk 4.27 lakh \[[30 (2020)](#o30)\]. It has no separate UAE figure: migrants to its "other countries" group, which covers the UAE, paid Tk 4.36 lakh \[[30 (2020)](#o30)\]. 71% paid a broker's fee \[[30 (2020)](#o30)\]; at village level the broker (dalal) recruits by word of mouth and takes payments even without a receipt \[[30 (2020)](#o30)\].

**How people pay.** 77.6% of migrants borrow to go: after relatives and friends, from family (28.2% of borrowers), NGOs (20.5%), moneylenders (15.3%) or against land (14.4%) \[[30 (2020)](#o30)\]. 28.2% of borrowers pay a set interest rate and 32.7% a fixed extra sum, and a quarter risk losing the collateral they pledged if they cannot repay \[[30 (2020)](#o30)\].

**What the job pays.** Dubai Taxi drivers earn a 35% commission per ride, about AED 2,500 to 4,000 a month (about Tk 85,000 to 1.36 lakh) \[[N4](#n4), [2](#o2)\]. In the BBS survey, workers earned on average Tk 23,629 a month (unskilled) and Tk 29,477 (skilled) in their first three months abroad \[[30 (2020)](#o30)\]. In a 2019 study 44% of low-skilled workers got less than promised \[[N20 (2019)](#n20)\].

> **Need to know**
> - What do UAE construction helpers, cleaners, security guards and salesmen actually take home in 2026, after food and accommodation deductions?

**How long the debt takes.** On the survey's averages, the cost equals 17.6 months of earnings; 19.1 months for men and 20.2 for unskilled workers \[[30 (2020)](#o30)\].

**What ties you to the employer**

Tied at every stage: only the employer can apply for, renew and cancel your visa, and it can cancel it online without telling you \[[14](#o14), [31](#o31), [N10](#n10)\].

- **To enter:** only the employer holding your MOHRE work permit can bring you in \[[14](#o14)\].
- **To renew:** the employer renews, every two years \[[32](#o32), [31](#o31)\].
- **To quit:** 30 to 90 days' written notice; in probation, 14 days to leave the UAE or one month to move to another employer \[[15](#o15), [10 (2021)](#o10)\].
- **To change employer:** allowed when the contract ends or is lawfully ended; quitting in probation against the rules, or a proven abandonment report, brings a one-year work ban (If you want to change employer) \[[15](#o15), [33](#o33)\]. For Bangladeshis, company-to-company transfers have been restricted since July 2024 \[[N11](#n11)\].
- **To leave:** the employer cancels the permit and residence; you then have 30 days to leave or change status \[[34](#o34)\].
- **Which law:** domestic workers come under their own law, not the main labour law; free-zone workers are under the free zone's rules together with the labour law \[[35 (2022)](#o35), [36](#o36)\].
- **Running away:** more than 7 days' absence lets the employer file a work abandonment report, which, if proven, brings a one-year ban, blocks any unemployment insurance payout while open, and Human Rights Watch says is used against workers fleeing abuse (If your employer reports you absent) \[[37 (2022)](#o37), [33](#o33), [38](#o38), [N31](#n31)\].
- **An expired visa:** AED 50 a day after the grace period (Going home without valid papers) \[[34](#o34)\].

**What goes wrong most, and what people wrongly believe**

**The commonest losses.** Losing the visa while on leave (2026); arriving on a visa with no job behind it (free visa, fake smart card); pay lower than promised; and being stuck with an employer you cannot leave \[[N7](#n7), [N2](#n2), [N29 (2022)](#n29), [N20 (2019)](#n20)\].

**What people believe, and the facts.**
- **"If I have a job, my visa is safe."** The employer can cancel it online without the worker knowing; check it before you fly (Going home on leave) \[[N10](#n10), [N14](#n14)\].
- **"A free visa lets me work anywhere."** No such visa exists; working outside the sponsoring company is illegal \[[N2](#n2)\].
- **"Go on a visit visa; the work visa gets done there."** A visit visa gives no right to work, and working on one brings fines \[[6](#o6)\].
- **"The UAE banned Bangladeshis from January 2026."** That claim came from a private visa website; the embassy said the site's Dubai address does not exist and most of its customers say they were cheated \[[N24](#n24), [16](#o16)\].
- **"A partner visa is a work visa."** It allows work only for your own licence \[[N3 (2019)](#n3)\].

**How irregular routes end.**
- **Amnesty:** over 50,000 Bangladeshis took the 2024 amnesty \[[N32](#n32)\].
- **Bans:** a Foreign Ministry official says a cancelled residence or a broken law bars re-entry for 5 to 10 years \[[N26 (2024)](#n26)\].
- **Protest:** jail, then deportation (above) \[[12 (2024)](#o12), [29 (2024)](#o29)\].

## Visas not open to Bangladeshis

**Status:** see Background ("Is it open now"); each visa below gives its own status line \[[N5](#n5), [N6](#n6)\].

- **Visa on arrival or visa-free entry:** not for Bangladeshis; only listed nationalities get it on arrival \[[7](#o7), [8](#o8)\].
- **14-day or 60-day visa for holders of a US, UK, EU or other approved residence or visa (including a US green card):** limited to nationals of India, Indonesia, Kenya, the Philippines, South Africa, Thailand and Vietnam; Bangladeshis are not eligible even with a US, UK or EU visa or residence \[[39](#o39), [40](#o40), [8](#o8)\].
- **Tourist visa applied at a UAE embassy:** UAE embassies, including Dhaka, do not issue tourist visas \[[41](#o41)\]. The embassy can issue courtesy visit visas, with ICP approval, to people it chooses and to diplomatic and UN passport holders \[[42 (2022)](#o42)\].
- **Converting a visit visa to a work visa inside the UAE:** closed to Bangladeshis for several years \[[N28](#n28)\]. A residence visa can only be processed after the person enters on a valid entry permit \[[6](#o6)\].

## Work visa: company worker

The standard work visa is a two-year residence visa, renewable, and only the employer can apply for it \[[14](#o14)\]. It rests on a Ministry of Human Resources and Emiratisation (MOHRE) work permit, which for a worker recruited from abroad is also valid two years, and on a fixed-term contract of at most three years \[[32](#o32), [9](#o9)\]. The employer must be registered with MOHRE and hold an approved quota of work permits \[[43](#o43), [5](#o5)\]. MOHRE sorts jobs into 9 skill levels; levels 6 to 9 (farm, craft and construction workers, machine operators and "simple professions") need no certificate \[[44](#o44), [43](#o43)\]. A job counts as skilled only at levels 1 to 5 with a post-secondary certificate, attested, and at least AED 4,000 (about Tk 1.36 lakh) a month \[[44](#o44), [2](#o2)\]. You must be at least 18 \[[32](#o32)\]. The law bars the employer from charging you any recruitment or employment cost, directly or indirectly; the work permit, the AED 3,000 MOHRE bank guarantee, the entry permit and the residence visa are the employer's costs \[[10 (2021)](#o10), [6](#o6), [5](#o5)\].

**Is recruitment open now?** See Background ("Is it open now"). In May 2025 MOHRE reopened online work-permit applications for skilled Bangladeshi workers, and BMET cleared 22,030 workers for the UAE in January to September 2026, more than twice the year before \[[N18](#n18), [N23](#n23)\].

> **Need to know**
> - Is MOHRE issuing new work permits for Bangladeshi workers in skill levels 6 to 9 as of November 2026, or only for skilled levels?
> - Does MOHRE's system currently block or refuse any work-permit applications because the worker is Bangladeshi?

### Step 1: Find a real job

1. **Register on the Overseas Employment Platform (OEP),** BMET's (Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) website for job-seekers. A UAE permission letter requires the agency to choose workers from BMET's database, and BMET clears no worker outside it \[[3](#o3)\].

   You'll need \[[45](#o45), [46](#o46)\]:

   - your NID (national ID card);
   - a mobile number;
   - Tk 200 for the government fee.

   1. Open [employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration](https://employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration) \[[46](#o46)\].
   2. Type your name in English, mobile and NID numbers, date of birth, division and district exactly as on the NID, the captcha, tick the terms and press "যাচাই করুন" (Verify) \[[45](#o45)\].
   3. Print 2 copies of the registration slip and give your fingerprints at the district office (DEMO, District Employment and Manpower Office) \[[46](#o46)\]. DEMO addresses are listed on BMET's website \[[47](#o47)\].

   **If it fails:** call the OEP hotline, 09614445271 \[[48](#o48)\].

2. **Learn a trade if you need one.** BMET lists every TTC (Technical Training Centre) and IMT (Institute of Marine Technology) with contacts on its website \[[49](#o49)\].

   **Women going as domestic workers** must do housekeeping training at one of 41 TTCs before going \[[50](#o50)\]. The course, its admission rules and the clearance age (25 to 45) are under Work visa: domestic worker, Steps 1 and 2 \[[50](#o50), [51](#o51)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - Is the UAE open to new Bangladeshi women domestic workers in 2026, given 764 women were cleared for the UAE from January to 7 November 2026 \[[52](#o52)\]?
   > - Does the UAE require any skill test or certificate from Bangladeshi workers before the visa, as Saudi Arabia does?

3. **Through a recruiting agency: find and check the permission letter.** For a UAE job order BMET issues a permission letter naming the agency, the employer, the Consulate's attestation number, each job and its pay, the conditions and the most the agency may take \[[3](#o3)\]. Example (Navira Ltd, recruiting licence RL-0712, 21 August 2025): Transguard Group LLC, Dubai; 470 men as masons (AED 1,150 a month), shutter carpenters and steel fixers (AED 1,050), electricians (AED 1,400), loaders and warehouse workers (AED 1,080), helpers and cleaners (AED 800); 2-year contract, 9 hours a day, 6 days a week; the company pays housing, transport, return air tickets and the visa, gives a food allowance and takes no recruitment fee; the agency may take at most Tk 71,840 \[[3](#o3)\].

   You'll need: the agency's RL number and the employer's name \[[53](#o53)\].

   1. Open [bmet.gov.bd/pages/miscellaneous-infos](https://bmet.gov.bd/pages/miscellaneous-infos) and search "আমিরাত", "Dubai" and your agency's RL number \[[53](#o53), [54](#o54)\].
   2. Open the PDF and compare the employer, job, pay and maximum fee with what you were told \[[3](#o3)\].
   3. Check the Consulate attestation number on the letter (it starts "BCG/DUBAI(LWW)") matches your demand letter \[[3](#o3), [55 (2023)](#o55)\].

   As of 8 November 2026 BMET's list shows only two UAE letters, from August 2023 and August 2025 \[[53](#o53), [54](#o54)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - Where does BMET publish permission letters for the 23,044 workers cleared for the UAE in 2026 \[[56](#o56)\]?
   > - Do individual (single) UAE visas get a permission letter at all?

4. **Have the demand letter and visa checked.** BMET checks job visas and demand letters free within 3 working days and checks UAE visas online; bring the visa and, if stamped, a copy of the passport page \[[50](#o50)\]. Do this at your DEMO or BMET, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka \[[57](#o57), [58 (2021)](#o58)\]. A UAE demand letter is valid only once the Consulate General in Dubai or the Embassy in Abu Dhabi has attested it \[[59 (2021)](#o59), [60](#o60)\]. To attest it, the employer gives the Consulate a demand letter attested by the UAE Chamber of Commerce, a power of attorney to the Bangladeshi agency, the recruitment contract, a guarantee letter to the Ministry's Secretary, a specimen work agreement, both companies' licences and the UAE company's quota \[[61](#o61)\]. The Consulate charges the company AED 221, cash, non-refundable, at Room 14 \[[62](#o62)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - Can a worker ask the Consulate or Embassy labour wing to confirm an attestation number, and how?

5. **Through a relative in the UAE (individual visa).** The Consulate's labour wing attests individual employment, residence and housemaid visas \[[59 (2021)](#o59), [60](#o60)\]. A worker with a visa they got themselves applies for clearance at BMET's emigration branch in Dhaka or the Chattogram DEMO (Step 6) \[[50](#o50)\]. Source tax on a single visa is Tk 250 if the visa is attested and Tk 400 if not \[[50](#o50)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - What does the Consulate or Embassy charge to attest an individual employment visa, and what does the sponsor bring?

**Before paying:**

- **Know the cap.** The government's UAE maximum is Tk 1,07,780, set in 2017 \[[11 (2019)](#o11)\]; the 2025 Navira letter set Tk 71,840 \[[3](#o3)\]. The worker pays nothing for the UAE side: the employer bears the levy and airfare \[[3](#o3)\].
- **Pay late, by bank.** The agency may take its money only once the visa, BMET clearance and flight are confirmed, by cheque, bank draft or pay order \[[3](#o3)\]; pay into the agency's bank account and keep receipts \[[57](#o57)\].
- **Get the contract and Bangla translation before clearance** \[[3](#o3)\].
- **Don't go on a visit visa to work:** the Ministry warns that brokers send people to the Middle East on tourist or visit visas with false job promises \[[63](#o63)\].
- **Borrow from PKB** (Probashi Kallyan Bank, the government bank for migrants) once you have a visa: up to Tk 3 lakh at 9% simple interest, repaid monthly over up to 3 years, decided within 7 working days \[[64](#o64)\].

  You'll need \[[64](#o64)\]:
  - the free bank form, 3 photos, NID copy;
  - passport, visa and BMET card copies;
  - 3 signed MICR cheque leaves from your own account with a statement;
  - a PKB savings account opened with at least Tk 500;
  - two guarantors: one financially solvent person with cheques, statement and proof of income, and one family member.

  You and the guarantors must live in the branch's area and you must be 18 to 60 \[[64](#o64)\]. Fees: 0.5% processing and 0.5% documentation, plus VAT, and Tk 900 per lakh risk cover \[[64](#o64)\]. Women can take PKB's women's migration loan, which needs one guarantor and no processing fee \[[65](#o65)\]. Apply at the branch or on mygov.bd, which sends application and tracking numbers by SMS \[[66 (2019)](#o66)\]. Helpline 16238, or +880 9677-787878 from abroad \[[64](#o64)\].

**If an agency or broker takes your money:** taking money on a false job promise or holding a migrant's passport is a crime punishable by up to 5 years' jail and a fine of at least Tk 1 lakh \[[67 (2023)](#o67)\]. Call the Probashi call centre, 16135 (toll free, run by the Wage Earners' Welfare Board) \[[68](#o68)\]; the Consulate in Dubai also forwards complaints to the District Commissioner in Bangladesh on its complaint form \[[69](#o69)\]. You can also complain in writing, with evidence, to BMET's Director General: free, decided within 30 working days; Director (Employment) 01711111544, diremi@bmet.gov.bd \[[50](#o50)\]. Free legal advice: 16699 \[[70](#o70)\].

> **Need to know**
> - Can a complaint against a recruiting agency be filed with BMET online, and which form does it use?

### Step 2: The job offer and contract (MOHRE offer letter)

1. **Get the MOHRE offer letter, not a company letter.** A real UAE job offer is an offer letter issued on MOHRE's system and signed by the company's authorised manager \[[6](#o6)\]. It must be in Arabic, English and a third language you understand, which can be Bengali, and comes with an annex summarising the Labour Law \[[9](#o9)\]. The employer signs it electronically and sends it to you in Bangladesh, directly or through a recruitment agency \[[9](#o9)\].

   **Check what it says.** The contract built from it must give the employer's name and address, your name, nationality and date of birth, your job, start date, workplace, working hours, rest days, probation, contract length, wage with allowances, annual leave, notice period and how the contract ends \[[71 (2022)](#o71)\]. After you sign, the employer may not change the offer without your consent \[[9](#o9)\]. An employer who gives MOHRE false information can be fined up to AED 20,000 \[[9](#o9)\].

2. **Sign it.** Workers in skill levels 1 to 3 sign; levels 4 and 5 may give a fingerprint \[[9](#o9)\]. The signed offer goes with the employer's application for the work permit (Step 3) \[[9](#o9)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - How do workers in skill levels 6 to 9 sign the offer: signature, fingerprint or through the agency?

3. **Check the offer is real before you pay anything.** MOHRE's enquiry site checks a job offer by its reference number \[[6](#o6)\].

   You'll need: the transaction (reference) number printed on the offer \[[6](#o6)\]; the form also has a box for the company's establishment number \[[72](#o72)\].

   1. Open [inquiry.mohre.gov.ae](https://inquiry.mohre.gov.ae/) \[[6](#o6)\].
   2. Choose "Inquire about job offers" (الاستعلام عن عرض العمل) \[[72](#o72), [73](#o73)\].
   3. Type the number in "Transaction number" and the company's number in "Establishment number" \[[6](#o6), [72](#o72)\]. The form also has a date range ("A field of history" is the from-date, "Field to date" the to-date, day/month/year) and a permit-type list set to "All work permits" \[[72](#o72)\]. Type the code shown in the picture and press "research" (search) \[[72](#o72)\].

   Also check the company exists: search its English and Arabic name on the National Economic Register, [ner.economy.ae](https://ner.economy.ae/) \[[6](#o6)\]. For questions about a contract or recruitment, MOHRE answers on +971 6 802 7666 or ask@mohre.gov.ae \[[6](#o6)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which fields of the MOHRE job-offer form are compulsory, is the establishment number printed on the offer, and what does the result show?

4. **On arrival, sign again; the offer becomes your contract.** You and the employer sign the same offer in the UAE and MOHRE registers it as the binding employment contract; no clause can change unless your rights are not reduced, both of you agree and MOHRE approves \[[9](#o9)\]. Contracts of workers in skill levels 6 to 9 are handed over at a workers' awareness (Tawjeeh) centre \[[74](#o74)\]. You can see your registered contract on MOHRE's enquiry site ("View attested employment contract", عرض عقد العمل الموثق) \[[73](#o73)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - Must the employer register the contract within 14 days of arrival (u.ae) or within 60 days (MOHRE's service page) \[[9](#o9), [74](#o74)\]?
   > - What does the Tawjeeh session cost in 2026, who pays it, and is it compulsory for levels 6 to 9 \[[N33 (2018)](#n33)\]?

5. **Know the probation rules.** Probation is at most six months; the employer must give 14 days' written notice to end it \[[75](#o75)\]. If you resign in probation to leave the UAE you give 14 days' notice; to move to another UAE employer, one month, and the new employer compensates your current employer for your recruitment cost unless you and your current employer agreed otherwise \[[75](#o75)\].

**Scam:** fraudsters sell "jobs" and "visas" for cash on social media and messaging apps under unlicensed company names or posing as official bodies \[[N34](#n34)\]. A visit or tourist visa never lets you work, and working on one brings fines for worker and employer \[[76](#o76)\].

### Step 3: The employer gets your work permit and entry permit

1. **The employer needs a MOHRE quota.** It opens a MOHRE file and gets an electronic quota of permits; MOHRE sets the number from the company's legal status, premises, projects and needs, after a Taq'eem site evaluation (AED 406) \[[5](#o5), [77](#o77)\].

2. **The employer applies for the work permit.** It uploads your photo, a passport copy valid at least six months, the offer signed by both of you and, for levels 1 to 5 only, your certificates \[[43](#o43)\]. You must not hold an active UAE work permit with another company \[[5](#o5)\]. MOHRE decides in 2 working days \[[43](#o43)\]. The fees, all the employer's, are \[[43](#o43), [10 (2021)](#o10)\]:

   - AED 50 application fee;
   - the two-year permit: AED 250 (category 1 company), AED 1,200 (category 2) or AED 3,450 (category 3, companies with violations) \[[32](#o32), [78](#o78)\];
   - at most AED 72 to a business (typing) centre for the paperwork \[[43](#o43)\];
   - an AED 3,000 bank guarantee, refunded when your labour card is cancelled \[[5](#o5)\].

   The approval is valid 60 days; if the employer doesn't pay the fees and guarantee in that time it is cancelled automatically \[[5](#o5)\].

   **Why it can be refused.** MOHRE refuses or cancels permits for false documents, a fictitious company or one not doing its licensed business, or one not paying wages through the Wage Protection System \[[32](#o32)\]. Companies accused of human trafficking or with unfit labour housing get no new permits \[[32](#o32)\].

   **The trap.** Within six months of issue the employer may switch the permit to another worker, even of another nationality, up to twice, as long as ICP cancels the first worker's entry permit \[[43](#o43)\]. A permit in your name can therefore be given to someone else \[[43](#o43)\].

3. **The entry permit.** With MOHRE's approval the employer gets an electronic employment entry permit from the residency authority: GDRFA (General Directorate of Identity and Foreigners Affairs) for Dubai, ICP (Federal Authority for Identity, Citizenship, Customs and Port Security) for the other six emirates \[[5](#o5), [6](#o6)\]. ICP issues it in 48 hours for AED 300 in fees plus a refundable AED 1,000 guarantee \[[79 (2024)](#o79)\]. The employer sends it to you electronically \[[6](#o6)\]. You must enter the UAE within 60 days of issue \[[80](#o80)\]. A company that is not really operating is fined AED 20,000 for using entry permits \[[80](#o80)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - Does the UAE Embassy in Dhaka have any role in employment entry permits for Bangladeshi workers?
   > - Is Bangladesh among the nationalities that must show an attested certificate of good conduct (police clearance) for a work entry permit \[[80](#o80)\]?

4. **Medical before you fly.** The UAE's own fitness test is done after arrival (Step 7) \[[5](#o5)\]. Wafid, the Gulf Health Council's booking system, lists the UAE as a destination for pre-departure medicals in Bangladesh, at $10 for a standard appointment \[[81](#o81)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - Do UAE employers or BMET require a Wafid (GAMCA) medical in Bangladesh for UAE work visas in 2026?

5. **How long.** In order:

   1. The employer gets its MOHRE quota (2 working days) \[[77](#o77)\].
   2. The work permit: 2 working days \[[43](#o43)\].
   3. The employer pays within 60 days \[[5](#o5)\].
   4. The entry permit: 48 hours \[[79 (2024)](#o79)\].
   5. You enter within 60 days \[[80](#o80)\].
   6. Medical, Emirates ID and residence within 60 days of entry (Step 7) \[[82 (2024)](#o82)\].

   Through the Work Bundle, MOHRE's single platform for permit, residence, medical and Emirates ID, the whole UAE side is meant to take 5 working days and two visits \[[32](#o32), [83](#o83)\].

> **Need to know**
> - Can a refused work permit or employment entry permit be appealed, and by whom (the employer through a MOHRE grievance, or an ICP or GDRFA reconsideration request)?

**What you get back:** an electronic entry permit for employment in your name, and a MOHRE work-permit approval you can check (Step 5) \[[6](#o6), [73](#o73)\].

### Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates

1. **Get an e-passport.** You fill the form online and submit it at the divisional or regional passport office for your present address \[[84](#o84)\].

   You'll need \[[84](#o84)\]:
   - your NID (compulsory over 20); fill the form exactly as on it; nothing needs attesting;
   - technical certificates such as a driving licence, uploaded with the form and shown in original at the office.

   1. Fill in the form online at epassport.gov.bd \[[84](#o84)\].
   2. Pay through A-Challan, DGePay or ShurjoPay, or at a bank \[[85](#o85), [86](#o86)\]. A 48-page passport costs Tk 4,025 / 6,325 / 8,625 for 5 years and Tk 5,750 / 8,050 / 10,350 for 10 years (regular / express / super express) \[[85](#o85), [86](#o86)\].
   3. Go to the passport office with the original NID and certificates \[[84](#o84)\]. Delivery is within 15, 7 or 2 working days of biometric enrolment \[[85](#o85), [86](#o86)\]. No police verification is needed with an online-verified NID \[[87](#o87)\].

   **If it fails:** passport helpline 16445 (09666716445 from abroad), 24 hours \[[88 (2023)](#o88)\]. If the passport is lost, file a GD (General Diary, a police report) at the nearest police station and re-apply with a copy of the old passport and the GD \[[84](#o84)\].
2. **Get a police clearance if it is required.** ICP asks for an attested certificate of good conduct for work entry only for "announced categories" \[[80](#o80)\].

   You'll need \[[89](#o89)\]:
   - a passport valid at least 3 months;
   - a present address that is the permanent or emergency address in the passport, where you live;
   - a scan of the passport data page attested by a first-class gazetted officer;
   - Tk 1,500 by treasury challan, code 1-7301-0001-2681, at any Bangladesh Bank or Sonali Bank branch, or online by card with a service charge.

   1. Register at [pcc.police.gov.bd](https://pcc.police.gov.bd/), log in and click "Apply" \[[89](#o89)\].
   2. Fill in your personal details, then your present and permanent addresses; police verify you at the present address \[[89](#o89)\].
   3. Upload the scanned documents \[[89](#o89)\].
   4. Track it by SMS: "PCC S <reference>" to 26969. Collect it with your passport or application copy, or send someone with an attested authorisation letter naming them and their NID number \[[89](#o89)\].

   **If it fails:** helpdesk Sunday-Thursday 9 am-5 pm, 01320001824, 01320001825, help.pcc@police.gov.bd \[[89](#o89)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which UAE jobs or emirates ask Bangladeshi workers for a police clearance?

3. **Certificates for a skilled UAE permit.** The chain is: issuing authority and Ministry of Education \[[90](#o90)\], then MoFA Bangladesh, then the UAE Embassy in Dhaka through UAE MOFA's "Attestation through UAE Missions Abroad" service \[[91](#o91)\].
   1. On mygov.bd, register, choose the attestation service for the document, upload it as JPEG/JPG/PNG and pay Tk 200 a page; it takes 5 working days and an SMS tells you when it is ready \[[92](#o92)\].
   2. The UAE does not accept Bangladesh's e-Apostille \[[92](#o92), [N35](#n35)\], so take the Apostille and documents to MoFA's consular counter, 10:00-13:00, for a consular sticker \[[92](#o92)\].
   3. Experience certificates must first be attested by BMET or the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and notarised; MoFA then attests them free \[[92](#o92)\].
   4. Apply to UAE MOFA online: sign in, choose "Attestation through UAE Missions Abroad", pay and note the reference number; if you can't apply online, go to the mission \[[91](#o91)\].

   **Check it is real:** UAE MOFA's digital verification, verify.mofa.gov.ae \[[91](#o91)\]. **If it fails:** UAE MOFA support +97180044444 \[[91](#o91)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - What does the UAE Embassy in Dhaka charge per certificate, and how long does it take?

### Step 5: Check your visa and permit are real

1. **The job offer and work permit:** on [inquiry.mohre.gov.ae](https://inquiry.mohre.gov.ae/), "Inquire about job offers" (filled in as in Step 2) and "Electronic work permit information" (معلومات تصريح العمل الإلكتروني) \[[6](#o6), [73](#o73), [72](#o72)\]. MOHRE's call centre is 600590000, 24 hours \[[43](#o43)\].
2. **The entry permit:** first see which emirate issued it \[[6](#o6)\].
   - *Dubai:* GDRFA's Visa Status page, [smart.gdrfad.gov.ae/Public_Th/StatusInquiry_New.aspx](https://smart.gdrfad.gov.ae/Public_Th/StatusInquiry_New.aspx) \[[93 (2024)](#o93)\]. The form is in Arabic. Choose "ملف" (file) and enter the file number, nationality (207 - بنجلاديش, Bangladesh) and date of birth; or choose "طلب" (application) and enter the application number, transaction number, payment date and first name in English \[[94](#o94)\]. Help: +971 4 313 9999 from abroad, 800 5111 in the UAE \[[95](#o95)\].
   - *Abu Dhabi, Sharjah, Ajman, Umm Al Quwain, Ras Al Khaimah, Fujairah:* ICP's "File Validity" page at [smartservices.icp.gov.ae](https://smartservices.icp.gov.ae/echannels/web/client/default.html#/fileValidity) \[[6](#o6), [96](#o96)\]. Leave "GCC Citizen" unticked \[[96](#o96)\]. Under "Search By" choose "File No." or "Passport Information", and under "Select the Type" choose "Residency", "Visa" or "Emirates ID" \[[96](#o96)\]. With "File No." and "Residency", give the "Emirate Unified Number", the file number or the Emirates ID number, then pick the nationality and type the date of birth (dd/MM/yyyy) \[[96](#o96)\]. Help: 600522222 \[[95](#o95)\].
3. **The company:** National Economic Register, [ner.economy.ae](https://ner.economy.ae/) (Step 2) \[[6](#o6)\].
4. **After arrival, your Emirates ID application:** at smartservices.icp.gov.ae with the application number starting 010 \[[83](#o83)\].

> **Need to know**
> - With "Visa" or "Passport Information" selected, which fields does ICP's File Validity form ask for, where is the "Emirate Unified Number" printed, and what does the result show for an employment entry permit?
> - Does the GDRFA form accept a search by passport number for an entry permit?

**If a check fails:** stop paying. Report it to MOHRE (+971 6 802 7666, ask@mohre.gov.ae) and, for a Dubai scam, to Dubai Police on 901 or its eCrime platform \[[6](#o6), [N34](#n34)\]. The Bangladesh-side complaint routes are in Step 1 ("If an agency or broker takes your money").

### Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance and the flight

1. **Do the pre-departure orientation (PDO), 3 days.** It is compulsory, at one of 70 BMET institutes; pay against a money receipt (Tk 200 at Feni TTC; pay no more) \[[50](#o50), [97](#o97)\]. Download the certificate at training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-certificate: type your passport or NID number and the captcha \[[98](#o98)\].
2. **Check the contract** at least 2 days before you go: job, employer, workplace, length, pay, leave and airfare \[[99 (2021)](#o99)\], against the permission letter (Step 1).
3. **BMET clearance (the smart card).** The agency applies with the demand letter, power of attorney, contract and skill certificates; BMET clears within 3 working days of the visa \[[3](#o3), [50](#o50)\]. Your costs:
   - welfare fee Tk 5,500 to the Wage Earners' Welfare Board from 1 October 2026 \[[100](#o100)\];
   - insurance Tk 1,000 once (5 years, Tk 10 lakh cover) \[[57](#o57)\];
   - clearance fee Tk 250 \[[50](#o50)\];
   - source tax Tk 1,200 (skilled) or Tk 800 (unskilled) on a group visa \[[50](#o50)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - Is the Tk 250 clearance fee still charged, given the Ministry reportedly abolished the smart card fee in December 2024 \[[N36 (2024)](#n36)\]?

   **Individual visa:** apply at BMET's emigration branch, Dhaka, or the Chattogram DEMO with your application, visa copy, any contract, attested OEP card copy, a Tk 300 stamp declaration and the pay order for the welfare fee and insurance \[[50](#o50)\]. Women on a single visa need a guardian's no-objection letter \[[99 (2021)](#o99)\].

   **Check it is real** at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check: type your passport number (or membership ID) and the captcha \[[101](#o101)\]; the airport welfare desks check smart cards \[[50](#o50)\]. Workers sent on fake smart cards got neither legal status nor jobs \[[N29 (2022)](#n29)\]. **If it fails:** BMET's Director (Emigration), +8802222222415, 01711-135901, diremi@bmet.gov.bd, or the OEP hotline 09614445271 \[[50](#o50), [48](#o48)\].
4. **The flight.** The employer pays the airfare, and an agency representative must fly with you or meet you at the UAE airport \[[3](#o3)\].
5. **Before you fly:** keep one set of copies at home and 3 with you, open 2 bank accounts, and never carry a packet you don't know \[[57](#o57)\]. Save the labour wing numbers (Contacts).

### Step 7: After arrival (medical fitness test, Emirates ID, residence, bank account, SIM)

1. **Finish everything within 60 days of entry.** The medical, Emirates ID and residence must be completed within 60 days of arrival or daily fines apply; a residence visa can't be processed while you are outside the UAE \[[82 (2024)](#o82), [6](#o6)\]. The fine is AED 50 a day \[[102](#o102)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - Is the deadline 60 days from entry (ICP) or one month (u.ae, "Preparing to work") \[[82 (2024)](#o82), [76](#o76)\]?

2. **Medical fitness test.** You must be found free of communicable diseases to get residence \[[103](#o103), [104](#o104)\]. The tests are HIV, hepatitis B, leprosy, syphilis and tuberculosis (TB), plus pregnancy for domestic workers \[[76](#o76)\]. Residency is refused if HIV or leprosy is positive \[[76](#o76)\]. TB scars or active or drug-resistant TB bring a conditional certificate and a one-year residence while you are treated in the UAE \[[103](#o103), [76](#o76)\]. Abu Dhabi uses a chest X-ray \[[103](#o103)\]; Emirates Health Services (EHS) centres do the laboratory tests and radiology (X-ray) examinations needed \[[104](#o104), [105](#o105)\].

   - *Dubai:* Dubai Health's 25 "Salem" medical fitness centres, including Al Quoz, Al Yalayis Jebel Ali, JAFZA (Jebel Ali Free Zone) and Al Muhaisnah; premium results in 6 to 24 hours \[[106](#o106)\]. EHS also runs residency medical centres in Dubai: Al Nahda, Salah El Deen, Ibn Battuta, Dragon Mart 2, Al Baraha Smart and Al Refaa \[[104](#o104)\].
   - *Sharjah, Ajman, Ras Al Khaimah, Fujairah, Umm Al Quwain:* EHS centres, for example Sahara and Al Khibra (Sharjah), Musheirif (Ajman), Dahan (Ras Al Khaimah), Mena Tower (Fujairah) and Al Madar (Umm Al Quwain) \[[104](#o104), [105](#o105)\]. Workers pay AED 250 plus the centre's printing fee \[[104](#o104)\]. Domestic workers, private drivers and nursery supervisors pay AED 350, including AED 50 vaccination for a new application and AED 50 pregnancy test; salon, barbershop, gym and health-facility workers pay AED 300, including AED 50 vaccination for a new application; both also pay the centre's printing fee \[[104](#o104)\]. Apply on the EHS website or app (log in with UAE Pass, the UAE's digital ID), by the EHS call centre, at a typing centre or at the centre; pay, arrive 20 minutes before the appointment, and get an electronic report within 24 hours \[[104](#o104)\]. EHS toll free: 800 8877 \[[104](#o104)\].

   You'll need: a passport photo, your passport and the entry permit copy \[[107](#o107), [104](#o104)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - What happens to a worker found HIV-positive or unfit: who pays the flight home, and is the worker detained first?
   > - What does the Abu Dhabi (Department of Health) visa screening cost?

3. **Emirates ID biometrics.** Your fingerprints and photo are taken for the Emirates ID as part of the same bundle \[[83](#o83)\]. The card costs AED 100 per year of residence, plus AED 100 e-service fee; a typing office adds AED 30 \[[79 (2024)](#o79)\].

4. **Residence visa.** ICP issues the work residence for two years: AED 100 application, AED 100 a year and AED 100 e-service fee; it needs the fit result and health insurance \[[79 (2024)](#o79)\]. These are employment costs the employer may not pass to you \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - Is the residence still printed in the passport for Dubai-issued visas, or shown only on the Emirates ID \[[5](#o5)\]?

5. **Contract and Tawjeeh.** Workers in skill levels 6 to 9 collect their MOHRE contract at a Tawjeeh (workers' awareness) centre (Step 2, item 4) \[[74](#o74)\]. Check it matches the offer you signed in Bangladesh \[[9](#o9)\].

6. **Keep your passport.** Possession of your own passport is your right \[[76](#o76)\]. If the employer keeps it, see "Your rights at work".

7. **Salary account.** Private-sector wages must be paid through the Wage Protection System (WPS), into a bank, exchange house or other institution licensed by the Central Bank \[[108](#o108)\]. A bank account needs your original Emirates ID and a salary letter, contract or payslip; Emirates NBD, for example, asks for a salary transfer of at least AED 5,000 \[[N37](#n37)\].

   > **Need to know**
   > - Which payroll cards or exchange-house accounts do employers use for workers earning under AED 5,000, and what do they charge?

8. **SIM card.** A normal prepaid or postpaid line needs your Emirates ID under the telecom regulator's (TDRA) rules \[[N38 (2022)](#n38)\]. Until it arrives, buy a visitor or tourist SIM on your passport (e& (Etisalat) visitor line: 90 days) and convert it once you have the Emirates ID \[[N38 (2022)](#n38)\].

### Your rights at work

Federal Decree-Law 33 of 2021 (the UAE Labour Law, in force since 2 February 2022) and its Executive Regulations (Cabinet Resolution 1 of 2022) cover private-sector workers; domestic workers have their own law \[[75](#o75), [10 (2021)](#o10), [71 (2022)](#o71), [109](#o109)\]. MOHRE (the Ministry of Human Resources and Emiratisation) enforces them \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\]. Free-zone workers come under the free zone's own regulations together with the Labour Law (see Work visa: free zone employee) \[[36](#o36)\].

**Contract.** Contracts are fixed-term and renewable by agreement; there are no open-ended contracts \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\]. You get one of the two copies \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\], in Arabic plus a language you understand (Arabic prevails in a dispute) \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\]. Its terms must match the MOHRE job offer you signed in Bangladesh \[[110](#o110)\]. MOHRE job offer forms carry a barcode serial number you can check on 600590000, the MOHRE website or app \[[111](#o111)\]. MOHRE's "Know Your Rights" guide exists in Bengali \[[110](#o110)\].

**No fees, no passport-keeping.** Recruitment, travel and residence-permit costs are the employer's \[[110](#o110)\]; charging workers recruitment fees is illegal \[[110](#o110)\]. The employer may not keep your passport or other official documents, and you need no employer permission to leave the country \[[110](#o110), [10 (2021)](#o10)\]. Forced work, threats, sexual harassment, bullying and violence are banned \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\].

**Hours and overtime.**
- 8 hours a day or 48 a week; 2 hours less in Ramadan \[[112](#o112), [71 (2022)](#o71)\].
- After 5 hours in a row, breaks totalling at least 1 hour, not counted as working hours \[[112](#o112)\].
- Overtime at most 2 hours a day, and total working hours (normal plus overtime) at most 144 every 3 weeks \[[112](#o112), [10 (2021)](#o10)\]; paid at the basic hourly rate plus 25%, or plus 50% between 10 pm and 4 am (not for shift workers) \[[112](#o112)\].
- Work on your rest day or a public holiday: another day off, or the day's pay plus at least 50% of basic \[[112](#o112), [113](#o113)\].

**Summer midday break.** No work in direct sun or open places from 12:30 pm to 3 pm, 15 June to 15 September every year \[[114](#o114)\]. Exempt: asphalt or concrete work that cannot wait, urgent public repairs, and permit-controlled work \[[114](#o114)\]. Employers must give shade, cooling, drinking water and first aid \[[115](#o115)\]. The fine is AED 5,000 per worker, up to AED 50,000 \[[116 (2023)](#o116)\]. Report a breach on 600590000, the MOHRE app or website ("Add Complaint") \[[115](#o115)\]; anyone can also report heat-rule, overtime, accommodation and safety breaches through MOHRE's community reporting service or 80084 \[[N39](#n39)\].

**Rest and holidays.** At least one paid rest day a week \[[113](#o113)\]. Paid public holidays: 1 January; Eid Al Fitr 1-3 Shawwal; Arafah Day; Eid Al Adha 10-12 Dhu al-Hijjah; Hijri New Year; the Prophet's birthday; National Day 2-3 December; Islamic dates follow moon sighting \[[117](#o117)\].

**Leave.**
- Annual: 30 days a year after one year; 2 days a month between 6 and 12 months \[[113](#o113)\]. The employer sets dates with one month's notice \[[113](#o113)\] and may not stop you taking it for more than two years in a row \[[113](#o113)\]. Unused leave is paid when you leave \[[113](#o113)\].
- Sick: none paid during probation \[[113](#o113)\]; afterwards up to 90 days a year, 15 full pay, 30 half pay, 45 unpaid \[[113](#o113)\]. Tell the employer within 3 days with a medical report \[[113](#o113)\]. You cannot be dismissed or given notice while on sick leave \[[113](#o113)\].
- Bereavement 5 days (spouse) or 3 days (parent, child, sibling, grandparent, grandchild) \[[113](#o113)\]; parental 5 working days within 6 months of a birth \[[113](#o113)\]; maternity 60 days (45 full, 15 half pay) \[[113](#o113)\]; Hajj up to 30 days unpaid, once per employer \[[113](#o113)\].

**Pay.** There is no minimum wage \[[108](#o108)\]. Wages must go through the Wage Protection System (WPS), MOHRE's system for paying private-sector salaries through banks, exchange houses or financial institutions authorised by the Central Bank \[[108](#o108)\]. Since Ministerial Resolution 340 of 2026, last month's wage is due on the 1st of each month, and at least 85% of the total due must be paid on time \[[108](#o108)\]. What happens to a late employer is under If you aren't paid.

**Deductions.** Only in the cases the law lists \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\]:
- loan repayments, with your written consent and no interest \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\];
- overpaid wages, up to 20% of the wage \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\];
- disciplinary fines under a MOHRE-approved list, up to 5% \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\];
- damage you caused, up to 5 days' wage a month (more only by court order) \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\];
- court debts, up to a quarter of the wage \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\].

All deductions together may never pass 50% of the wage \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\].

**Housing.** The employer must give licensed housing, or pay a housing allowance, or include it in the wage \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\]; firms with 50 or more workers earning AED 1,500 a month or less must house them \[[118](#o118)\]. Standards:
- at least 3 square metres per person, 8 to 10 people per room at most \[[118](#o118)\];
- your own bed, side table and 2-metre lockable cupboard; no three-tier bunks \[[118](#o118)\];
- one toilet and one shower per 8 people \[[118](#o118)\];
- air-conditioning in every room \[[118](#o118)\].

> **Need to know**
> - Is there any limit on what an employer may charge for housing or food when housing is "included in the wage"?

**Health insurance.** The employer must insure you: since 1 January 2025 a policy is required before your residence permit is issued or renewed; the scheme is mandatory in Abu Dhabi and Dubai and is being extended to the other five emirates \[[31](#o31)\]. The basic package costs AED 320 a year, with no waiting period for chronic illness \[[31](#o31)\]; you pay at most AED 100 per outpatient visit (25% co-pay) and AED 500 per inpatient stay, capped at AED 1,000 a year \[[31](#o31)\]. In Dubai a worker earning AED 4,000 or less must get at least the Essential Benefits Plan, and the employer may not deduct the premium or cut pay for it \[[119 (2015)](#o119)\].

> **Need to know**
> - Where does a worker complain if the employer deducts the health insurance premium outside Dubai?

**Unemployment insurance (ILOE, Involuntary Loss of Employment).** Compulsory for private-sector workers (not domestic workers, temporary-contract workers or under-18s) \[[38](#o38)\]. You pay it yourself: up to AED 5 a month if your basic salary is less than AED 16,000 \[[38](#o38)\], through iloe.ae, the ILOE app, Tasheel or Tawjeeh centres (MOHRE's typing and service centres), Al Ansari Exchange, bank apps, du or Etisalat SMS or BOTIM \[[38](#o38)\]. Not joining, or not paying, costs a AED 400 fine \[[38](#o38)\]; after 3 months unpaid the policy is cancelled \[[120](#o120)\]. Check your policy at iloe.ae: choose your sector, enter Emirates ID and mobile, request the OTP (one-time password sent by SMS), then View Policy Details \[[120](#o120)\]. Claiming is under If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job.

**Worker protection insurance.** When hiring, the employer buys a policy (AED 137.50 for skilled, AED 180 for low-skilled workers) or lodges a AED 3,000 bank guarantee \[[121](#o121)\]. If the firm goes bankrupt or will not pay, it covers up to AED 20,000 per worker: up to the last 120 days' unpaid wages, gratuity, the ticket home, body repatriation and work-injury medical costs \[[121](#o121)\]. MOHRE draws on it in a proven collective dispute without the employer's consent \[[37 (2022)](#o37)\].

> **Need to know**
> - How does an individual worker claim the worker protection insurance: through MOHRE only, or directly with the insurer?

**Gratuity (end-of-service pay).** After at least one year: 21 days' basic wage per year for the first five years, 30 days' per year after that, pro rata for part years, capped at two years' wage \[[122](#o122)\]. Basic wage only; housing and transport allowances do not count \[[122](#o122)\]. Unpaid absence does not count as service \[[122](#o122)\]; the employer may deduct money you owe \[[122](#o122)\].

> **Need to know**
> - Does a foreign private-sector worker get any pension or social-security contribution in the UAE, or only the end-of-service gratuity, and can low-wage workers join MOHRE's voluntary end-of-service savings scheme?

### How long you can stay

**Two years at a time.** A work permit for a worker recruited from abroad lasts two years \[[32](#o32)\]; sponsored residence visas run 1, 2 or 3 years \[[102](#o102)\]. Renewal is the employer's job and needs the health insurance above \[[31](#o31)\].

**The 180-day rule.** A residence visa dies automatically after more than 180 days in a row outside the UAE \[[102](#o102)\]. Exceptions include residents sent abroad for treatment with a UAE-approved medical report, students and investors, plus anyone ICP (the Federal Authority for Identity, Citizenship, Customs and Port Security) exempts for a fee \[[102](#o102)\]; ICP issues a permit to stay outside more than six months for a valid reason \[[82 (2024)](#o82)\]. If the visa lapsed while you were abroad for work, study or treatment, you can apply from abroad for an entry permit, with a reason, paying AED 100 per 30 days outside, and must enter within 30 days of approval; this is not available to Dubai residents \[[102](#o102)\].

> **Need to know**
> - Is there an upper age limit for a worker's work permit or residence renewal?

**When the job ends.** After cancellation you have a grace period, 30 days for ordinary workers, to change status or leave \[[34](#o34), [15](#o15)\]; after that overstay costs AED 50 a day \[[34](#o34), [123](#o123)\]. Leaving with unpaid fines needs ICP's departure permit, used within 7 days \[[124](#o124)\].

**Cancelled while on leave.** Since July 2026 many Bangladeshis' visas were cancelled while they were at home on leave (see Going home on leave) \[[N7](#n7)\].

### What can get you jailed

**Papers and work.**
- Working without a work permit is banned \[[42 (2022)](#o42)\]; using a visa for a purpose other than the one it was granted for: jail or a fine of at least AED 10,000, and deportation \[[125 (2021)](#o125)\]. Whoever employs or houses you illegally pays AED 50,000, and you are deported \[[125 (2021)](#o125)\].
- Forged visas or residence permits: up to 10 years and deportation \[[125 (2021)](#o125)\]. Entering illegally: jail and deportation \[[125 (2021)](#o125)\].
- Overstay is fined AED 50 a day \[[123](#o123)\]; not paying can bring up to 3 months' jail or a fine of up to AED 4,000, and the court may order deportation \[[125 (2021)](#o125)\].

**Protests.** Joining a public gathering of 5 or more people that disturbs order and not leaving when told: at least 1 year and AED 100,000; at least 5 years if it disrupts security, traffic or work \[[126](#o126)\]. Calling for unpermitted demonstrations online: jail and/or AED 200,000 to 1 million \[[127](#o127)\]. In July 2024, 57 Bangladeshis who protested in UAE streets got 10 years to life; they were pardoned and deported in September 2024 \[[12 (2024)](#o12), [29 (2024)](#o29)\]. Recording and sharing videos of the gatherings counted against them \[[12 (2024)](#o12)\]. Joining a strike or non-peaceful protest also cancels any ILOE claim \[[38](#o38)\].

**Social media and insults.**
- False news or rumours online: at least 1 year and AED 100,000 \[[127](#o127)\].
- Insulting someone online: jail and/or AED 250,000 to 500,000 \[[127](#o127)\].
- Photographing people without consent, or posting photos of accident victims: at least 6 months and/or AED 150,000 to 500,000 \[[127](#o127)\].
- Insulting someone by phone or in front of others: up to 6 months or AED 5,000; publicly, up to 1 year or AED 20,000 \[[126](#o126)\].

**Drugs.** Using drugs: at least 3 months' jail or a AED 20,000 to 100,000 fine for a first offence; a third offence at least 2 years and AED 100,000 \[[128](#o128)\]. Since December 2025, foreigners convicted of any narcotics offence must be deported after the sentence \[[129](#o129)\].

> **Need to know**
> - Does a positive blood or urine test alone, with drugs taken outside the UAE, count as "use" for prosecution?

**Alcohol.** Legal only where emirate rules allow \[[126](#o126)\]. Drinking in public or unlicensed places, or being drunk and causing trouble in public: up to 6 months and/or a fine \[[126](#o126)\]. Being drunk at work allows dismissal without notice \[[15](#o15)\].

**Relationships.** Consensual sex outside marriage: at least 6 months for both, but only on a complaint by a spouse or guardian \[[126](#o126)\]. Having a child outside marriage: at least 2 years for both unless they marry or acknowledge the child and get it papers \[[126](#o126)\].

**Other.** Begging: up to 3 months and AED 5,000 \[[126](#o126)\]. Assault causing under 20 days' injury: up to 1 year and AED 10,000 \[[126](#o126)\]. A bounced cheque for lack of funds has been a civil matter since January 2022, but an unpaid cheque debt can bring a travel ban and frozen assets, and a cheque on a closed account or with fraud is still criminal \[[N40](#n40)\].

**After arrest.** The Bangladesh labour wing visits jails and deportation centres \[[60](#o60)\]. For a worker with no valid passport the Embassy issues a Travel Permit (out pass): a form giving passport number, sponsor, arrival date, cause of jail and sentence, with two photos \[[130](#o130), [131](#o131)\]. Family can call the Abu Dhabi labour wing (+971 2 445 8182, labourwingauh@gmail.com) or the Dubai consulate's humanitarian line +971 56 847 1066 \[[132](#o132), [133](#o133)\].

> **Need to know**
> - Does the Embassy or consulate get notified when a Bangladeshi is arrested, and how does a family find which jail holds a worker?

### If you aren't paid

**First collect proof.** Keep photos of your contract, MOHRE job offer, Emirates ID, passport, WPS bank or card statements and any written messages; MOHRE asks for documents supporting the claim, such as a resignation or dismissal letter \[[134](#o134)\]. Complain within 30 days of the breach \[[135 (2023)](#o135)\].

**What MOHRE does to a late employer** (Resolution 340 of 2026) \[[108](#o108)\]:
- day 2 after the due date: alerts to the employer;
- day 5: no new work permits for it;
- day 11: an administrative fine, and for a repeat within six months the firm is moved to the third category, which pays the highest permit fee \[[108](#o108), [32](#o32)\];
- day 16: labour disputes opened automatically for unpaid workers in firms of 25 or more;
- day 21: an executive (enforceable) order to pay in firms under 50 workers, or a collective labour dispute in firms of 50 or more; attachment of assets and a travel ban on the person in charge; prosecution for repeat offenders with 50 or more \[[108](#o108)\].

**1. Confidential salary complaint.** "My Salary Complaint" on the MOHRE website or app does not tell the employer who complained; free \[[136](#o136)\]. You must be registered in MOHRE's database and have no pending complaint or court case; it takes 14 working days \[[136](#o136)\].

**2. Formal labour complaint.**

You'll need \[[134](#o134)\]:
- passport number, name, date of birth, nationality;
- Emirates ID number;
- a mobile number for the OTP;
- any supporting papers.

1. Open "Register Labour Complaints - Private Sector Employees" on mohre.gov.ae or the MOHRE UAE app; filing by phone is only for UAE citizens \[[134](#o134), [137](#o137)\]. For help, call 600590000 (24 hours; also WhatsApp) or the Labour Claims and Advisory Centre on 80084 (toll free) \[[43](#o43), [111](#o111), [137](#o137)\].
2. Enter your details and the employer's; confirm with the OTP sent by SMS or email \[[134](#o134)\].
3. MOHRE calls both sides to settle; if that fails it sets a meeting and texts you the date, then tries a second time \[[134](#o134)\].

What you get back: a settlement, or for claims under AED 50,000 a MOHRE decision that is enforceable like a court order \[[135 (2023)](#o135)\]; either side can go to court within 15 working days, which suspends it \[[135 (2023)](#o135)\]. Over AED 50,000 without settlement, the case goes to court \[[135 (2023)](#o135)\]. MOHRE aims to finish within 14 days; the service is free \[[135 (2023)](#o135), [134](#o134)\]. During the dispute MOHRE may make the employer keep paying wages for up to two months \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\]. If you are fired for a serious complaint or valid case, the court can award up to 3 months' wage on top of notice pay and gratuity \[[15](#o15)\].

If MOHRE cannot reach the employer within 5 working days (10 if it asks for time), it cancels your permit on your request so you can move, and late-permit fines go on the employer's file \[[37 (2022)](#o37)\]. You can also quit without notice, keeping your rights, if you notify MOHRE 14 working days ahead and the employer does not fix the breach \[[15](#o15)\].

**3. Labour court.** Register the case at court within 14 days of MOHRE's referral \[[37 (2022)](#o37)\]. If either side takes a MOHRE decision to court, the court sets a hearing within 3 working days and decides within 30 \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\]. No court fees on claims under AED 100,000 \[[137](#o137)\]; there are legal-support offices for workers in the courts \[[110](#o110)\]. While the case runs you may get a temporary permit with a new employer, unless you were reported absent \[[37 (2022)](#o37)\]; if you stopped working, your permit is cancelled 6 months after referral \[[37 (2022)](#o37)\]. Claims cannot be heard after two years from the end of the job \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\].

**4. The labour wing.** Abu Dhabi: +971 2 446 5100 or +971 2 445 8182, labourwingauh@gmail.com \[[132](#o132)\]. Dubai consulate (Al Wuheida, Deira): 04-2388202 or 04-2690764, labourwingdubai@gmail.com \[[133](#o133)\]. It takes complaints, visits camps and attends labour court \[[60](#o60)\].

**In practice:** Human Rights Watch reports wage theft, recruitment fees and passport confiscation continuing, with Bangladeshi workers among those hit, and false absconding charges filed against workers who leave abuse \[[N31](#n31)\]. Workers cannot form unions or protest \[[N41](#n41)\].

> **Need to know**
> - How long do MOHRE complaints by Bangladeshi workers actually take, and how many end in payment?
> - Does the labour wing accompany workers to MOHRE meetings, and does it charge anything?
> - Does MOHRE still run 80060 (option 2 for domestic workers) for checking a recruitment office, alongside 80084 and 600590000?

### If you want to change employer

**The old bans.** Before 2011 an employer could refuse a no-objection certificate (NOC), forcing the worker to leave the UAE for six months; under the 2011 rules unskilled and semi-skilled workers still needed the employer's consent to move in their first two years \[[N42 (2011)](#n42)\]. Today the law lets you move in the cases below, and the only ban is one year \[[15](#o15), [33](#o33)\].

**When you can move freely** \[[15](#o15)\]:
- the contract expired and was not renewed;
- it was ended lawfully, including by you without notice for the employer's breach, assault or danger \[[15](#o15)\];
- the employer ended it without fault on your part.

**Quitting with notice.** Either side may end the contract for a legitimate reason with 30 to 90 days' written notice; whoever skips it pays the other the notice-period wage \[[15](#o15)\]. If the employer gives notice, you may take one unpaid day a week to job-hunt, telling them 3 days ahead \[[15](#o15)\].

**During probation (up to 6 months).** To move to another UAE employer give one month's written notice; the new employer repays the old one's recruitment costs unless agreed otherwise \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\]. To leave the UAE give 14 days' notice \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\]. Breaking these rules means no work permit for a year from departure \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\].

**How the move happens.**

1. **Get a new MOHRE job offer.** The new employer issues it on MOHRE's system and you both sign it (Step 2) \[[6](#o6), [138](#o138)\]. Check it at inquiry.mohre.gov.ae, "Inquire about job offers" (Step 2, item 3) \[[6](#o6)\].
2. **Make sure the old permit is cancelled.** You may not hold an active work permit when the new one is applied for, and the new employer must apply within 90 days of the cancellation \[[138](#o138)\].
3. **The new employer applies for the transfer work permit.** All skill levels qualify and it takes 2 working days \[[138](#o138)\]. It is free on MOHRE's website and app apart from the federal fee (AED 50 for categories 1-3); business centres may charge up to AED 72 a service \[[138](#o138)\].

   You'll need \[[138](#o138)\]:
   - a clear colour photo on a white background;
   - a passport copy valid at least six months;
   - the MOHRE job offer signed by you and the new employer;
   - an academic certificate for skill levels 1-5 (none for levels 6 to 9).

4. **Check before you start work.** The job must fit the company's licensed activities, and its licence must be valid with no violations that suspend it \[[138](#o138)\]; look the company up on ner.economy.ae (Step 2) \[[6](#o6)\]. MOHRE notifies when the permit is issued; follow it on inquiry.mohre.gov.ae ("Electronic work permit information"), the MOHRE app or 600590000 \[[138](#o138), [73](#o73)\]. Do not work for the new firm before the permit \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\].

**If it fails:** if the old employer won't cancel the permit, file a labour complaint ("If you aren't paid"); MOHRE cancels the permit without the employer's consent if it cannot reach the employer within 5 working days (extendable by 5) \[[37 (2022)](#o37)\].

**The one-year ban** applies if you quit during probation without the employer breaching, if a work abandonment report is proven, or if your permit at a fake company is cancelled \[[33](#o33)\]. It runs from your departure and lifts by itself \[[33](#o33)\]. Where the ban is for a proven work abandonment report, it does not apply to family-sponsored workers, a new permit with the same firm, workers at skill levels the UAE needs, or Golden visa holders \[[33](#o33)\]. Check for one on 600590000 (8 am-8 pm, several languages); contest it with a grievance and documents through MOHRE channels \[[33](#o33)\].

### If your employer reports you absent (work abandonment)

**What it is.** A "work abandonment" report (the old "absconding" case) can be filed after more than 7 days' absence in a row when the employer cannot locate or contact you \[[37 (2022)](#o37)\]. Absence over 7 consecutive days, or 20 in a year, also allows dismissal without notice \[[15](#o15)\]. Dismissal without notice needs a written investigation and a written, reasoned decision \[[15](#o15)\].

**What follows.** A proven report means no new work permit for a year \[[33](#o33)\], no temporary permit during a court case \[[37 (2022)](#o37)\], no ILOE payout while it is open \[[38](#o38)\], and removal from WPS \[[108](#o108)\]. Human Rights Watch says false reports are used against workers who leave abuse, risking detention and deportation \[[N31](#n31)\].

**Grounds to cancel it** \[[37 (2022)](#o37)\]:
- 7 days had not passed, or you had a legitimate reason;
- you were at work or on lawful leave;
- the employer agreed you could stop or look for another job;
- the employer agrees to take you back.

**How to cancel it.**
1. Call MOHRE on 600590000 (24 hours; also WhatsApp) and ask whether a work abandonment report is on your file \[[43](#o43), [111](#o111)\].
2. On the MOHRE website or app, use "Cancellation of an Absence from Work Complaint (Absconding Report)": enter your work permit or passport number, name, date of birth, nationality, the absence date and the company details; confirm by OTP \[[139](#o139)\].
3. Attach proof of the ground you rely on, such as proof you were at work or on approved leave, or that the employer let you stop or look for another job \[[139](#o139), [37 (2022)](#o37)\].

What you get back: an SMS with the result within 14 working days; free \[[139](#o139)\]. If you left over unpaid wages or abuse, file the labour complaint first (If you aren't paid) \[[15](#o15)\].

> **Need to know**
> - Does a worker get an SMS or other notice when an employer files a work abandonment report?
> - Does a report block the worker's exit at the airport, and how is it cleared if the worker wants to go home?

### Leaving at the end of the job

1. **Notice and last pay.** Serve the notice in your contract \[[15](#o15)\]. Within 14 days of the end the employer must pay all wages, unused leave and gratuity \[[122](#o122), [113](#o113)\]. Ask for a free experience certificate \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\].
2. **Ticket home.** The employer pays unless you joined another employer or the termination was your fault \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\].
3. **Housing.** Leave employer housing within 30 days unless agreed otherwise \[[10 (2021)](#o10)\].
4. **Cancellation.** The employer cancels the work permit and residence; you then have 30 days' grace to leave or change status, or 90 days if you are a skilled worker at MOHRE level 1-3 \[[34](#o34)\]. If the employer will not cancel, MOHRE can cancel on your complaint (If you aren't paid) \[[37 (2022)](#o37)\]. To cancel, the employer needs your signature on the labour contract cancellation and on a letter confirming you received all wages and end-of-service benefits \[[102](#o102)\].
5. **ILOE, if you were let go** (If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job).

Claims under the labour law cannot be heard more than two years after the job ends, and an ILOE payout needs you to be in the UAE \[[10 (2021)](#o10), [38](#o38)\].

### Going home on leave

**Before you book.** Agree the dates in writing; the employer sets leave dates with a month's notice \[[113](#o113)\]. A worker who does not come back on time without a good reason is unpaid for the absence, and over 7 days can bring dismissal or an abandonment report \[[10 (2021)](#o10), [15](#o15), [37 (2022)](#o37)\].
